The perfected person is the wise one has attained the goal of yoga. He has achieved all that which has to be be achieved. His mind is not turned towards external objects, nor can he perform actions in the external world. Such is the nature of savikalpa samādhi, considered in association with the objects of knowledge.

Verse 866

866

शुद्धोऽहं बुद्धोऽहं प्रत्यग्-रूपेण नित्य-सिद्धोऽहम्। शान्तोऽहम् अनन्तोऽहं सतत-परानन्द-सिन्धुर् एवाहम्॥

Speaking of the Ātman, the Śruti says, ‘I am the pure, inmost Ātman, untouched by ignorance, and ever existent. I am at once tranquil and limitless. I am an ocean of eternal bliss.

Verse 867

867

आद्योऽहम् अनाद्योऽहं वाङ्-मनसा साध्य-वस्तु-मात्रोऽहम्। निगम-वचो-वेद्योऽहम् अनवद्याखण्ड-बोध-रूपोऽहम्॥

I have existed prior to all else. I am without a beginning. All which may be comprehended by means of words and by the mind, that I am. I am comprehended only by the Śruti. Pure knowledge, perfect and indivisible – such is my nature. ((KenU.1.5) (Kena Upaniṣad))

Verse 868

868

विदिताविदितान्योऽहं माया-तत्-कार्य-लेश-शून्योऽहम्। केवल-दृग्-आत्मकोऽहं संविन्-मात्रः सकृद्-विभातोऽहम्॥

I differ from the known and I am other than the unknown. I am free from Māyā and all its effects. I am the witnessing consciousness; I am pure knowledge, ever effulgent.

Verse 869

869

अपरोऽहम् अनपरोऽहं बहिर् अन्तश् चापि पूर्ण एवाहम्। अजरोऽहम् अक्षरोऽहं नित्यानन्दोऽहम् अद्वितीयोऽहम्॥

I am neither the effect nor the cause of anything. Within me and around me there is nothing other than the infinite Ātman. Old age, decay and death are not for me. I am imperishable, for I am Bliss Eternal; I am the one without a second. ((BrhUEng.2.5.19) (Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad))

Verse 870

870

प्रत्यग्-अभिन्नम् अखण्डं सत्य-ज्ञानादि-लक्षणं शुद्धम्। श्रुत्यवगम्यं तथ्यं ब्रह्मैवाहं परं ज्योतिः॥

I am Brahman which is at once the supreme light, and the supreme reality, and which is in no way other than the innermost Ātman, which is indivisible, and ever pure, which is of the nature of sat-cid-ānanda and which is comprehended only by what the Śruti says.

Verse 871

871

एवं सन्-मात्र-गाहिन्या वृत्त्या तन्-मात्र-गाहकैः। शब्दैः समर्पितं वस्तु भावयेन् निश्चलो यतिः॥

It is in this way that the man of renunciation should meditate through all the diverse modifications of the mind, upon the nature of pure existence as the sole object of thought, in so far as it is indirectly presented through the words of the Śruti.

Verse 872

872

कामादि-दृश्य-प्रविलाप-पूर्वकं शुद्धोऽहम् इत्यादिक-शब्द-मिश्रः। दृश्येव निष्ठस्य य एष भावः शब्दानुविद्धः कथितः समाधिः॥

In the first instance, one begins with the following concept: ‘I am the witness of the desires that arise in me’. Then step by step, one comes to realize, as pointed out by the Śruti. ‘I am pure’. It is in this way that one should meditate upon the Ātman with entire devotion. Such is the process by which one attains savikalpa samādhi, in association with the words of the Śruti.

Verse 873

873

दृश्यस्यापि च साक्षित्वात् समुल्लेखनम् आत्मनि। निवर्तक-मनोऽवस्था निर्विकल्प इतीर्यते॥

Nirvikalpa samādhi Where the mind functions (the reality of the vṛttis is) no longer, where there are no (real) objects of knowledge, and where the Ātman, the witnessing consciousness, is the sole reality, there arises the experience of nirvikalpa samādhi.

Verse 874

874

सविकल्प-समाधिं यो दीर्घ-कालं निरन्तरम्। संस्कार-पूर्वकं कुर्यान् निर्विकल्पोऽस्य सिध्यति॥

One who has practised savikalpa samādhi for a long time becomes at long last fit to attain nirvikalpa samādhi, by virtue of the residual impression of what he has practised previously, at an earlier stage.

Verse 875

875

निर्विकल्पक-समाधि-निष्ठया तिष्ठतो भवति नित्यता ध्रुवम्। उद्भवाद्यपगतिर् निर्-अर्गला नित्य-निश्चल-निरस्त-निर्वृतिः॥

It is certain that one who is solely intent upon the realization of Brahman in nirvikalpa samādhi becomes the eternal Brahman. For such the vicious cycle of birth and death has come to an end. Such a person attains the bliss that knows no obstacles, but which is eternal in nature, changeless and continuous.

Verse 876

876

विद्वान् अहम् इदम् इति वा किञ्चिद् बाह्याभ्यन्तर-वेदन-शून्यः। स्वानन्दामृत-सिन्धु-निमग्नस् तूष्णीम् आस्ते कश्चिद् अनन्यः॥

Rare indeed are the knowers of Brahman; for they are not other than Brahman. They are not conscious of anything within them such as the idea of ‘I and mine’; nor are they conscious of anything outside them such as ‘this or that’. They are lost, as it were, in the infinite ocean of bliss, which is the bliss of the inmost Ātman, and they remain for ever absorbed in a grand silence.

Verse 877

877

निर्विकल्पं परं ब्रह्म यत् तस्मिन्न् एव निष्ठिताः। एते धन्या एव मुक्ता जीवन्तोऽपि बहिर्-दृशाम्॥

They are indeed the most fortunate who have lost their individuality in that Supreme Being. They alone are liberated, even though they might seem to be (walking, talking) embodied beings as far as the others are concerned.

Verse 878

878

यथा समाधि-त्रितयं यत्नेन क्रियते हृदि। तथैव बाह्य-देशेऽपि कार्यं द्वैत-निवृत्तये॥

All these three types of samādhi, the savikalpa either in association with the objects of knowledge, or with the Śruti, and the nirvikalpa should be practised diligently. By such diligent observance, all that is within oneself, and all that which is without become negated; and it is only then that all duality comes to an end.

Verse 879

879

तत्प्रकारं प्रवक्ष्यामि निशामय समासतः। अधिष्ठानं परं ब्रह्म सच्-चिदानन्द-लक्षणम्॥

I shall now recapitulate briefly the nature of samādhi. Listen with attention. The Supreme Brahman is of the nature of existence-knowledge-bliss and is the substratum of all.

Verse 880

880

तत्राध्यस्तम् इदं भाति नाम-रूपात्मकं जगत्। सत्त्वं चित्त्वं तथानन्द-रूपं यद् ब्रह्मणस् त्रयम्॥

The universe of name and form seems to exist only in so far as it is superimposed upon Brahman. In reality, it is upon the triune nature of Brahman as sat-cid-ānanda that they have their being.

Verse 881

881

अध्यस्त-जगतो रूपं नाम-रूपम् इदं द्वयम्। एतानि सच्-चिद्-आनन्द-नाम-रूपाणि पञ्च च॥

In addition to those three, two different attributes are added from the world of phenomena viz. name and form. All these five entities become blended together. Thus name, form, existence, consciousness and bliss become intermingled.

Verse 882

882

एकी-कृत्योच्यते मूर्खैर् इदं विश्वम् इति भ्रमात्। शैत्यं श्वेतं रसं द्राव्यं तरङ्ग इति नाम च॥

Those who are unenlightened, mix together the real (existence, consciousness and bliss) and the unreal (name and form): and they refer to the combination of the two as ‘this universe’. It is as though one should put together the diverse factors such as coldness, whiteness, taste, liquidity, and the name ‘wave’ and describe it all together and say, ‘This is a wave’.

Verse 883

883

एकी-कृत्य तरङ्गोऽयम् इति निर्दिश्यते यथा। आरोपिते नाम-रूपे उपेक्ष्य ब्रह्मणस् ततः॥

It is only by superimposing the name (nāma) and the form (rūpa) that people say, ‘This is a wave’. It is in the same way that people superimpose name and form upon Brahman also. But when these superimpositions are taken away, what remains is Brahman alone.

Verse 884

884

स्व-रूप-मात्र-ग्रहणं समाधिर् बाह्य आदिमः। सच्-चिद्-आनन्द-रूपस्य सकाशाद् ब्रह्मणो यतिः॥

Therefore, a man of renunciation should separate name and form from Brahman, in order to realize Brahman which is of the nature of existence-consciousness-bliss by means of concentration.

Verse 885

885

नाम-रूपे पृथक् कृत्वा ब्रह्मण्येव विलापयन्। अधिष्ठानं परं ब्रह्म सच्-चिद्-आनन्दम् अद्वयम्। यत् तद् एवाहम् इत्येव निश्चितात्मा भवेद् ध्रुवम्॥

It is in this way that by merging everything in Brahman that the man of renunciation becomes convinced, and knows for certain, ‘I am undoubtedly the Supreme Brahman, non-dual, of the nature of existence-knowledge-blīss, and that which constitutes the substratum of all names and forms.

Verse 886

886

इयं भूर् न सन् नापि तोयं न तेजो न वायुर् न खं नापि तत्-कार्य-जातम्। यद् एषाम् अधिष्ठान-भूतं विशुद्धं सद् एकं परं सत् तद् एवाहम् अस्मि॥

Neither earth, nor water, nor fire, nor air, nor space, nor their products constitute Brahman. As for me, I am none other than the non-dual Brahman, who is pure existence and the substratum of all.

Verse 887

887

न शब्दो न रूपं न च स्पर्शको वा तथा नो रसो नापि गन्धो न चान्यः। यद् एषाम् अधिष्ठान-भूतं विशुद्धं सद् एकं परं सत् तद् एवाहम् अस्मि॥

I am neither sound, nor form, nor touch, nor smell, nor taste, nor even anything other than these. I am none other than the supreme non-dual Brahman, who is pure existence and the substratum of all.

Verse 888

888

न सद् द्रव्य-जातं गुणा न क्रिया वा न जातिर् विशेषो न चान्यः कदापि। यद् एषाम् अधिष्ठान-भूतं विशुद्धं सद् एकं परं सत् तद् एवाहम् अस्मि॥

Neither the dravyas, nor the guṇas, nor the kriyās, nor jāti, nor viśeṣa, nor anything else constitute Brahman. Therefore I am none other than the supreme non-dual Brahman who is pure existence and the substratum of all.

Verse 889

889

न देहो न चाक्षाणि न प्राण-वायुर् मनो नापि बुद्धिर् न चित्तं ह्यहन्-धीः। यद् एषाम् अधिष्ठान-भूतं विशुद्धं सद् एकं परं सत् तद् एवाहम् अस्मि॥

I am neither the body, nor the senses, nor the vital force, nor the mind, nor the intellect, nor the thinking faculty, nor the ego. I am none other than the non-dual Brahman who is pure existence and the substratum of all.

Verse 890

890

न देशो न कालो न दिग् वापि सत् स्यान् न वस्त्वन्तरं स्थूल-सूक्ष्मादि-रूपम्। यद् एषाम् अधिष्ठान-भूतं विशुद्धं सद् एकं परं सत् तद् एवाहम् अस्मि॥

Neither space, nor time, nor the directions, nor other things, nor the gross world, nor the subtle constitute Brahman. I am none other than the non-dual Brahman, who is pure existence, and is the substratum of all.

Verse 891

891

एतद् दृश्यं नाम-रूपात्मकं यो ऽधिष्ठानं तद् ब्रह्म सत्यं सद् एति॥ गच्छंस् तिष्टन् वा शयानोऽपि नित्यं कुर्याद् विद्वान् बाह्य-दृश्यानुविद्धम्॥

The wise man who is ever absorbed in the practice of concentration, and to whom the whole of the external world of knowledge becomes blended in the consciousness of Brahman, knows at all times, whether he is walking, or standing or lying down, that in reality the universe of name and form is none other than Brahman, who alone exists and is the substratum of all.

Verse 892

892

अध्यस्त-नाम-रूपादि-प्रविलापेन निर्मलम्। अद्वैतं परमानन्दं ब्रह्मैवास्मीति भावयेत्॥

One should therefore meditate in the following manner; ‘I am Brahman, the non-dual Supreme Bliss’. One should merge in Brahman all name and form, for they are superimposed on Brahman.

Verse 893

893

निर्विकारं निराकारं निरञ्जनम् अनामयम्। आद्यन्त-रहितं पूर्णं ब्रह्मैवाहं न संशयः॥

That unchanging, formless, untainted Brahman, who knows no sorrow and who is without a beginning and an end, and is all-pervading, That I am; and there is no doubt about it.

Verse 894

894

निष्कलङ्कं निरातङ्कं त्रिविध-च्छेद-वर्जितम्। आनन्दम् अक्षरं मुक्तं ब्रह्मैवास्मीति भावयेत्॥

One should meditate in the following manner: ‘I am Brahman, who is taintless, who is free from fear; and also free from the three kinds of limitations imposed by time, space and the nature of things, and who is ever blissful, imperishable and free.’

Verse 895

895

निर्विशेषं निराभासं नित्य-मुक्तम् अविक्रियम्। प्रज्ञानैक-रसं सत्यं ब्रह्मैवास्मीति भावयेत्॥

One should therefore meditate in the following manner: ‘I am Brahman who is free from all attributes, whose nature is not reflected in anything else, who is ever free, who undergoes no change, and who is of the nature of pure knowledge, and of nothing else, and who is ever existing.’

Verse 896

896

शुद्धं बुद्धं तत्त्व-सिद्धं परं प्रत्यग् अखण्डितम्। स्व-प्रकाशं पराकाशं ब्रह्मैवास्मीति भावयेत्॥

One should therefore meditate thus: ‘I am Brahman, who is ever pure, who is untainted by ignorance; who is realizable only by means of the right knowledge of Brahman, who is the supreme, the inmost and indivisible, the self-effulgent.’

Verse 897

897

सुसूक्ष्मम् अस्तिता-मात्रं निर्विकल्पं महत्तमम्। केवलं परमाद्वैतं ब्रह्मैवास्मीति भावयेत्॥

One should therefore meditate in the following manner: ‘I am the supreme non-dual Brahman, whose nature is very subtle, who is pure existence, the absolute, the greatest of the great and ever pure.’

Verse 898

898

इत्येवं निर्विकारादि-शब्द-मात्र-समर्पितम्। ध्यायतः केवलं वस्तु लक्ष्ये चित्तं प्रतिष्ठति॥

One who thus meditates upon that pure reality, whose nature is indicated by such expressions as being devoid of change and the like, has one’s mind established in Brahman, the goal.

Verse 899

899

ब्रह्मानन्द-रसावेशाद् एकी-भूय तद्-आत्मना। वृत्तेर् या निश्चलावस्था स समाधिर् अकल्पकः॥

That firm state in which the intellect is absorbed in the bliss of Brahman and has become identical with the real nature of Brahman is known as nirvikalpa samādhi. lt is called ecstasy, or deep concentration at the absolute plane.

Verse 900

900

उत्थाने वाप्यनुत्थानेऽप्यप्रमत्तो जितेन्द्रियः। समाधि-षट्कं कुर्वीत सर्वदा प्रयतो यतिः॥

A man of renunciation should therefore give up all carelessness; he should have firm control over his senses, and practise with diligence the six types of concentration, whether he is standing or lying down.

Verse 901

901

विपरीतार्थ-धीर् यावन् न निःशेषं निवर्तते। स्व-रूप-स्फुरणं यावन् न प्रसिध्यत्यनर्गलम्। तावत् समाधि-षट्केन नयेत् कालं निरन्तरम्॥

One should therefore spend all one’s time in the unceasing practice of all these six varieties of concentration, until all the wrong ideas as to the nature of the Ātman have been eliminated, and until there is an uninterrupted realization of one’s ultimate nature.

Verse 902

902

न प्रमादोऽत्र कर्तव्यो विदुषा मोक्षम् इच्छता। प्रमादे जृम्भते माया सूर्यापाये तमो यथा॥

While engaged in the practice of concentration, the learned aspirant after liberation should be on guard against being careless. The reason is that when one is careless, Māyā settles down like the onset of darkness at nightfall.

Verse 903

903

स्वानु-भूतिं परित्यज्य न तिष्ठन्ति क्षणं बुधाः। स्वानु-भूतौ प्रमादो यः स मृत्युर् न यमः सताम्॥

The wise one should not therefore rest even for a moment or abstain from the pursuit of the knowledge of the Ātman. To a good man, Yama is not the cause of death, but it is indifference regarding the knowledge of the Ātman that brings about his death.

Verse 904

904

अस्मिन् समाधौ कुरुते प्रयासं यस् तस्य नैवास्ति पुनर् विकल्पः। सर्वात्म-भावोऽप्यमुनैव सिध्येत् सर्वात्म-भावः खलु केवलत्वम्॥

But if one should endeavour to practise concentration in the prescribed manner, none of the doubts can assail him. It is only in this way that one could attain sarvātma-bhava, and realize the unity of the Ātman which underlies all beings. To be aware of this oneness is indeed the culmination of spiritual realization.

Verse 905

905

सर्वात्म-भावो विदुषो ब्रह्म-विद्या-फलं विदुः। जीवन्-मुक्तस्य तस्यैव स्वानन्दानुभवः फलम्॥

The knowledge of Brahman brings with it the realization of the unity of the Ātman that underlies all beings; so say the learned. One who has attained Brahman remains a jīvan-mukta, liberated in life, and such a person realizes as a result of it the bliss of the Ātman.

Verse 906

906

योऽहं-ममेत्याद्यसद्-आत्म-गाहको ग्रन्थिर् लयं याति स वासनामयः। समाधिना नश्यति कर्म-बन्धो ब्रह्मात्म-बोधोऽप्रतिबन्ध इष्यते॥

It is in the stage of samādhi that all the knots of the heart are cut asunder. These so-called knots of the heart are only compact forms of desire, and are the nature of the ideas of ‘I and mine’. They are the sources of unreal knowledge. When they are cut asunder, one’s bondage of the past comes to an end. At the same time all the obstacles that lie in the path of the unity of the Ātman and Brahman come to to an end. ((ChanU.8.12.3) (Chāndogya Upaniṣad))

Verse 907

907

एष निष्कण्टकः पन्था मुक्तेर् ब्रह्मात्मना स्थितेः। शुद्धात्मनां मुमुक्षूणां यत् सद्-एकत्व-दर्शनम्॥

Only that path by travelling along which one comes to realize that the Ātman is identical with Brahman, is free from the thorns of life. By no other path could the aspirant reach the goal, the realization of pure consciousness.

Verse 908

908

तस्मात् त्वं चाप्यप्रमत्तः समाधीन् कृत्वा ग्रन्थिं साधु निर्दह्य युक्तः। नित्यं ब्रह्मानन्द-पीयूष-सिन्धौ मज्जन् क्रीडन् मोदमानो रमस्व॥

Be thou ever more intent upon practising the deep concentration of samādhi. Burn up once for all the obstacles that are caused by delusion. Take your fill of delight in the bliss of the eternal which is the nectar of immortality.

Verse 909

909

निर्विकल्प-समाधिर् यो वृत्तिर् नैश्चल्य-लक्षणा। तम् एव योग इत्याहुर् योग-शास्त्रार्थ-कोविदाः॥

Yoga or union with Brahman It is only in the state of nirvikalpa samādhi that all the ripples of thought that arise in the mind are stilled and there reigns complete tranquility. Such is the state of yoga or union with Brahman, according to those who are well versed in the yoga śāstra. ((MunU.2.2.7) (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad))

Verses 910–912

910–912

अष्टावङ्गानि योगस्य यमो नियम आसनम्। प्राणायामस् तथा प्रत्याहारश् चापि च धारणा॥ ध्यानं समाधिर् इत्येव निगदन्ति मनीषिणः। सर्वं ब्रह्मेति विज्ञानाद् इन्द्रिय-ग्राम-संयमः॥ यमोऽयम् इति सम्प्रोक्तोऽभ्यसनीयो मुहुर् मुहुः। सजातीय-प्रवाहश्च विजातीय-तिरस्-कृतिः॥

The eight constituent aspects of yoga There are eight constituent aspects of yoga. They are respectively known as yama, niyama, āsana, prāṇāyāma, pratyāhāra, dhāraṇa, dhyāna and samādhi. The wise say that the control of the senses that follows from the knowledge that all is Brahman… …That is indeed yama, or self-restraint and they say that this should be practised persistently. When the current of thought flows constantly in one direction, when it is seen that all are forms of the one Ātman, and when all other thoughts are excluded…

Verses 913–914

913–914

नियमो हि परानन्दो नियमात् क्रियते बुधैः। सुखेनैव भवेद् यस्मिन्न् अजस्रं ब्रह्म-चिन्तनम्॥ आसनं तद् विजानीयाद् इतरत् सुख-नाशनम्। चित्तादि-सर्व-भावेषु ब्रह्मत्वेनैव भावनात्॥

…That state is known as niyama. It is in this way that the learned derive supreme bliss. That state of mind in which there is a continuous stream of meditation upon Brahman is known as āsana, and it is this that leads to the destruction of all other pleasures.

Verse 915

915

निरोधः सर्व-वृत्तीनां प्राणायामः स उच्यते। निषेधनं प्रपञ्चस्य रेचकाख्यः समीरणः॥

When all the thoughts that arise in the mind are held under restraint by the one idea that all other factors such as the mind and the like are nothing but Brahman, that indeed is known as prāṇāyāma. In such prāṇāyāma, the negation of the universe is to be regarded as the equivalent of rechaka or the exhaling of the breath.

Verse 916

916

ब्रह्मैवास्मीति या वृत्तिः पूरको वायुर् ईरितः। ततस् तद्-वृत्ति-नैश्चल्यं कुम्भकः प्राण-संयमः॥

When the idea ‘I am Brahman’ permeates all the modifications of the mind it is the equivalent of pūraka or the inhaling of breath. When all the modifications of the mind are stilled, that is the state of kumbaka, or the equivalent of the retention of the breath.

Verse 917

917

अयं चापि प्रबुद्धानाम् अज्ञानां प्राण-पीडनम्। विषयेष्वात्मतां त्यक्त्वा मनसश् चिति मज्जनम्॥

The enlightened make use of kumbaka in order to retain the vital force, whereas the ignorant merely inflict pain upon themselves by resorting to it. To withdraw the mind from all the objects of knowledge, and to cease to identify oneself with them by making the mind merge in pure consciousness…

Verse 918

918

प्रत्याहारः स विज्ञेयोऽभ्यसनीयो मुमुक्षुभिः। यत्र यत्र मनो याति ब्रह्मणस् तत्र दर्शनात्॥

…This is known as pratyāhāra. Every aspirant after liberation should diligently practise it. To hold the mind under restraint and to look upon all that the mind conceives as Brahman…

Verse 919

919

मनसो धारणं चैव धारणा सा परा मता। ब्रह्मैवास्मीति सद्-वृत्त्या निरालम्बतया स्थितिः॥

…Is to be deemed as the highest type of dhāraṇa. To remain in the highest stage of awareness, unsupported by any external agency, through which there runs the one idea, ‘I am Brahman’ which permeates the whole mind…

Verse 920

920

ध्यान-शब्देन विख्याता परमानन्द-दायिनी। निर्विकारतया वृत्त्या ब्रह्माकारतया पुनः॥

…Should be regarded as dhyāna, and this is known to all. Dhyāna is also the giver of the highest type of bliss. When all the modifications that take place in the mind have been completely effaced, and when the mind is filled with an unchanging awareness of the form of Brahman…

Verses 921–922

921–922

वृत्ति-विस्मरणं सम्यक् समाधिर् ध्यान-संज्ञिकः। समाधौ क्रियमाणे तु विघ्ना ह्यायान्ति वै बलात्॥ अनुसन्धान-राहित्यम् आलस्यं भोग-लालसम्। भयं तमश्च विक्षेपस् तेजः-स्पन्दश्च शून्यता॥

…That indeed is the stage of samādhi, the fulfilment of meditation. As is well-known, there are many obstacles in the path of yoga. The lack of enquiry into the nature of Brahman, laziness, the desire for enjoyment, fear, ignorance, distraction, a sense of trembling felt all over the body, and a feeling of the void or śūnya, all these constitute some of the obstacles which intervene during the practice of yoga.

Verse 923

923

एवं यद् विघ्न-बाहुल्यं त्याज्यं तद् ब्रह्मविज्-जनैः। विघ्नान् एतान् परित्यज्य प्रमाद-रहितो वशी। समाधि-निष्ठया ब्रह्म साक्षाद् भवितुम् अर्हसि॥

Every aspirant after the knowledge of Brahman should overcome all these obstacles. He should avoid them by ceasing to be careless, by the restraint of the senses, and by the exclusive devotion to the practice of concentration in samādhi. In this way, may you become fit to be Brahman itself.

Verse 924

924

इति गुरु-वचनाच् छ्रुति-प्रमाणात् परम् अवगम्य स्व-तत्त्वम् आत्म-युक्त्या। प्रशमित-करणः समाहितात्मा क्वचिद् अचलाकृतिर् आत्म-निष्ठितोऽभूत्॥

The student’s direct experience gained by means of right knowledge With his senses made tranquil, with his mind controlled and body motionless, the disciple sat somewhere, and devoted himself exclusively to the pursuit of the Ātman. In the light of what the teacher said, and supported by the evidence of the Śruti he came to realize the supreme truth of the Ātman as himself.

Verse 925

925

बहु-कालं समाधाय स्व-स्व-रूपे तु मानसम्। उत्थाय परमानन्दाद् गुरुम् एत्य पुनर् मुदा॥

After having spent a long time in such ecstasy, and after having attained self-realization, the disciple came down to the normal plane of consciousness. He approached his guru, radiant in ineffable bliss…

Verses 926–927

926–927

प्रमाण-पूर्वकं धीमान् सगद्गदम् उवाच ह। नमो नमस् ते गुरवे नित्यानन्द-स्वरूपिणे॥ मुक्त-सङ्गाय शान्ताय त्यक्ताहन्ताय ते नमः। दया-धाम्ने नमो भूम्ने महिम्नः पारम् अस्य ते। नैवास्ति यत्-कटाक्षेण ब्रह्मैवाभवम् अद्वयम्॥

And spoke to him thus in a voice chocked with emotion. “O Revered Sir, guided by you, I have known that which may be realized only by means of the Śruti. O my preceptor! you are the embodiment of bliss eternal. Deign to accept this, my salutation to you. To you who are devoid of all attachment, who are tranquil, who have transcended the ego-consciousness, and who are the abode of grace, I offer my salutation. Your glory envelops the world. There is nothing that is greater than that. One gracious look from you is enough to make me one with the non-dual Brahman.

Verse 928

928

किं करोमि क्व गच्छामि किं गृह्णामि त्यजामि किम्। यन् मया पूरितं विश्वं महा-कल्पाम्बुना यथा॥

What is there for me to do? Whither shall I go? What is there for me either to accept or to reject? I find that the whole universe is like an infinite expanse of water at the end of great cycles, and that is enveloped by the Ātman within me.

Verse 929

929

मयि सुख-बोध-पयो-धौ महति ब्रह्माण्ड-बुद्बुद-सहस्रम्। मायामयेन मरुता भूत्वा भूत्वा पुनस् तिरोधत्ते॥

In that ocean of immortality, in that sea of consciousness, mighty worlds rise and roll away due to Māyā, like the scudding foam drifting before the wind.

Verse 930

930

नित्यानन्द-स्वरूपोऽहम् आत्माहं त्वद्-अनुग्रहात्। पूर्णोऽहम् अनवद्योऽहं केवलोऽहं च सद्-गुरो॥

Thanks to the grace that has been bestowed by you, I have realized the Ātman. I have realized bliss eternal. I am the infinite, innermost Ātman, which is all-pervading, taintless and pure.

Verse 931

931

अकर्ताहम् अभोक्ताहम् अविकारोऽहम् अक्रियः। आनन्द-घन एवाहम् असङ्गोऽहं सदा-शिवः॥

I am neither the doer, nor the enjoyer of the fruits of action. I know no change, I am not the product of any kind of creative activity. I am compact of bliss. I am the Ātman, the one without a second, and the ever-auspicious.

Verse 932

932

त्वत्-कटाक्ष-वर-चान्द्र-चन्द्रिकापात-धूत-भवतापज-श्रमः। प्राप्तवान् अहम् अखण्ड-वैभव- आनन्दम् आत्म-पदम् अक्षयं क्षणात्॥

Like a weary traveller tramping under the midday sun, I have been wandering through the cyclic changes of birth and death; and your benignant glance has been to me as refreshing as moonlight to the weary. I have attained in an instant the knowledge of the imperishable Ātman, and realized its inherent glory and bliss indivisible.

Verse 933

933

छायया स्पृष्टम् उष्णं वा शीतं वा दुष्ठु सुष्ठु वा। न स्पृशत्येव यत्-किञ्चित् पुरुषं तद्-विलक्षणम्॥

None of these things which are external, such as heat and cold, or good and bad, ever affect me; for, they are but reflex forms of pure consciousness, whereas the nature of the Ātman is altogether different from all of them.

Verse 934

934

न साक्षिणं साक्ष्य-धर्मा न स्पृशन्ति विलक्षणम्। अविकारम् उदासीनं गृह-धर्माः प्रदीपवत्॥

I am one with that witnessing consciousness whose nature is not stained by what it perceives. It is like a lamp that throws its light indifferently on the diverse objects in a home, itself remaining unaffected by that which it illumines.

Verse 935

935

रवेर् यथा कर्मणि साक्षि-भावो वह्नेर् यथा वायसि दाहकत्वम्। रज्जोर् यथारोपित-वस्तु-सङ्गस् तथैव कूटस्थ-चिद्-आत्मनो मे॥

Like unto the sun who is the sole witness of all that takes place under his light or like a red-hot ball of iron as it burns its way, although it is not the iron as such that burns, and like unto a rope which out of superimposition is mistaken to be something else, I am that pure consciousness which is untainted and which remains unchanged, although whatever is manifest is superimposed upon it.

Verse 936

936

इत्युक्त्वा स गुरुं स्तुत्वा प्रश्रयेण कृतानतिः। मुमुक्षोर् उपकाराय प्रष्टव्यांशम् अपृच्छत॥

After having offered to the guru his need of praise as a token of his abounding grace, the disciple bowed low and offered his salutation to his master. He then requested his preceptor to enlighten him further on the various types of liberation, for the benefit of all spiritual aspirants.

Verse 937

937

जीवन्-मुक्तस्य भगवन्न् अनुभूतेश्च लक्षणम्। विदेह-मुक्तस्य च मे कृपया ब्रूहि तत्त्वतः॥

Be pleased to enlighten me, Revered Sir, and give me the right kind of knowledge as to the nature of the experience attained in jīvan-mukti, or liberation in life, and how it differs from videha-mukti or liberation that endures when the body has been shuffled off.

Verse 938

938

ज्ञान-भूमिका-लक्षणम् वक्ष्ये तुभ्यं ज्ञान-भूमिकाया लक्षणम् आदितः। ज्ञाते यस्मिंस् त्वया सर्वं ज्ञातं स्यात् पृष्टम् अद्य यत्॥

The seven planes of the mind The spiritual guide says: At the outset, I shall define for you the various levels of consciousness, which lead ultimately to the right knowledge. By knowing it, whatever you have asked will also be known.

Verse 939

939

ज्ञान-भूमिः शुभेच्छा स्यात् प्रथमा समुदीरिता। विचारणा द्वितीया तु तृतीया तनु-मानसी॥

The first step to right knowledge is the desire to know, and it is known as the good intention. The second step is reflection, and the third is the diminution of all attachment felt by the mind toward external objects.

Verse 940

940

सत्त्वापत्तिश् चतुर्थी स्यात् ततोऽसंसक्ति-नामिका। पदार्थाभावना षष्ठी सप्तमी तुर्यगा स्मृता॥

The realization of sattva-guṇa is the fourth step. The next, the fifth, is non-attachment, and the sixth is the absence of the consciousness of all external objects. The seventh and the last step is that which leads to the stage of the turya, or the transcendent.

Verse 941

941

स्थितः किं मूढ एवास्मि प्रेक्ष्योऽहं शास्त्र-सज्जनैः। वैराग्य-पूर्वम् इच्छेति शुभेच्छा चोच्यते बुधैः॥

The good intention As soon as there is an awakening of vairāgya, one feels, ‘How ignorant I am, whereas the wise and good are well versed in the Śāstras’. That is the beginning of the desire to learn, and it is called the good intention.

Verse 942

942

शास्त्र-सज्जन-सम्पर्क-वैराग्याभ्यास-पूर्वकम्। सद्-आचार-प्रवृत्तिर् या प्रोच्यते सा विचारणा॥

The stage of reflection When as a result of being dispassionate one tends to practise good conduct, resorts to holy company and studies the Śāstras, it constitutes the second step and it is known as the stage of reflection.

Verse 943

943

विचारणा-शुभेच्छाभ्याम् इन्द्रियार्थेषु रक्तता। यत्र सा तनुताम् एति प्रोच्यते तनु-मानसी॥

Diminished mental attachment That plane in which all attachment to external objects drop off, where sense pleasure diminishes, as a result of the ripening of good intention and reflection, is known as the plane of diminished mental attachment.

Verse 944

944

भूमिका-त्रितयाभ्यासाच् चित्तेऽर्थ-विरतेर् वशात्। सत्त्वात्मनि स्थिते शुद्धे सत्त्वापत्तिर् उदाहृता॥

The realization of sattva In the fourth plane the mind attains purity and turns away from all objects of desire. There arises a sense of indifference as a cumulative result of the virtues practised in the three earlier stages. The fourth stage is therefore known as the plane of the realization of sattva.

Verse 945

945

दशा-चतुष्टयाभ्यासाद् असंसर्ग-फला तु या। रूढ-सत्त्व-चमत्-कारा प्रोक्ता संसक्ति-नामिका॥

The plane of non-attachment The fifth plane is that in which sattva is unusually predominant. This comes as a result of the virtues practised in the earlier stages, and it leads to vairāgya. It is known as the plane of non-attachment.

Verse 946

946

भूमिका-पञ्चकाभ्यासात् स्वात्मारामतया भृशम्। आभ्यन्तराणां बाह्यानां पदार्थानाम् अभावनात्॥

The total absence of the consciousness of external objects The sixth is the plane of padārtha-abhāvanā. In that stage there is a total absence of the consciousness of external objects. This stage is arrived at when the earlier stages become perfected, and when one is so deeply absorbed in the bliss of the Ātman that one does not feel that there is anything either within or without.

Verse 947

947

पर-प्रयुक्तेन चिर-प्रयत्नेनावबोधनम्। पदार्थाभावना नाम षष्ठी भवति भूमिका॥

When one is at that stage of consciousness, it is only as the result of prolonged effort on the part of others that one may be brought down, with great difficulty, to the normal plane of consciousness.

Verse 948

948

षड्-भूमिका-चिराभ्यासाद् भेदस्यानुपलम्भनात्। यत् स्व-भावैक-निष्ठत्वं सा ज्ञेया तुर्य-गा गतिः॥

Establishing in one’s own ultimate nature In this way we come to the seventh plane in which one is finally established in one’s own ultimate nature. This stage is the resultant of sustained effort maintained through all the earlier stages. Here, all consciousness of difference is totally lost. This leads to turya, which transcends waking, dreaming and deep sleep.

Verse 949

949

इदं ममेति सर्वेषु दृश्य-भावेष्वभावना। जाग्रज्-जाग्रद् इति प्राहुर् महान्तो ब्रह्मवित्तमाः॥

The waking aspect of waking consciousness To the mahātmā, who is the best of the knowers of Brahman, all such ideas as ‘this’ or ‘mine’ do not arise; for there is a total absence of the consciousness of external objects. It is the waking aspect of waking consciousness.

Verse 950

950

विदित्वा सच्-चिद्-आनन्दे मयि दृश्य-परम्-पराम्। नाम-रूप-परित्यागो जाग्रत्-स्वप्नः समीर्यते॥

The dream aspect of waking consciousness When one rejects all name and form after coming to know that all objects of knowledge are but superimpositions upon one’s own Ātman, whose nature is existence-consciousness-bliss, that is the dream aspect of waking consciousness.

Verse 951

951

परिपूर्ण-चिद्-आकाशे मयि बोधात्मतां विना। न किञ्चिद् अन्यद् अस्तीति जाग्रत्-सुप्तिः समीर्यते॥

The deep-sleep aspect of waking consciousness When one comes to know that nothing exists except pure consciousness, which permeates every thing as the paripūrṇa-cid-ākāśa as the all-permeating consciousness, infinite as space, that is the deep-sleep aspect of waking consciousness.

Verse 952

952

मूलाज्ञान-विनाशेन कारणाभास-चेष्टितैः। बन्धो न मेऽतिस्वल्पोऽपि स्वप्न-जाग्रद् इतीर्यते॥

The waking aspect of dream consciousness With the destruction of mūlāvidyā or the primordial ignorance, one comes to know that all actions are but the product of causes that are unreal, and which are only apparent. At that stage, when one is no longer bound even to the slightest extent, it is to be regarded as the waking aspect of dream consciousness.

Verse 953

953

कारणाज्ञान-नाशाद् यद् द्रष्टृ-दर्शन-दृश्यता। न कार्यम् अस्ति तज् ज्ञानं स्वप्न-स्वप्नः समीर्यते॥

The dream aspect of dream consciousness The distinction between the seer, the process of seeing and the seen is all due to ignorance. When ignorance is destroyed, knowledge destroys the effects of ignorance. That is the dream aspect of dream consciousness.

Verse 954

954

अतिसूक्ष्म-विमर्शेन स्व-धी-वृत्तिर् अचञ्चला। विलीयते यदा बोधे स्वप्न-सुप्तिर् इतीर्यते॥

The deep sleep aspect of dream consciousness When the intellect becomes calm and motionless, as a result of subtle reflection it becomes merged in pure consciousness, it is to be regarded as the deep sleep aspect of dream consciousness.

Verse 955

955

चिन्मयाकार-मतयो धी-वृत्ति-प्रसरैर् गतः। आनन्दानुभवो विद्वन् सुप्ति-जाग्रद् इतीर्यते॥

The waking aspect of deep sleep O Learned one, when as a result of the expanding of the mind the individual intellect becomes one with pure intelligence, the bliss of the Ātman is attained. The realization of that bliss is the waking aspect of deep sleep.

Verse 956

956

वृत्तौ चिरानुभूतान्तरानन्दानुभव-स्थितौ। समात्मतां यो यात्येष सुप्ति-स्वप्न इतीर्यते॥

The dream aspect of deep sleep When as a result of the prolonged experience of the bliss within, the mind ceases to function and all its modifications are no longer perceived, one comes to realize a state which is similar to that of the Ātman. That is to be regarded as the dream aspect of deep sleep.

Verse 957

957

दृश्य-धी-वृत्तिर् एतस्य केवली-भाव-भावना। परं बोधैकतावाप्तिः सुप्ति-सुप्तिर् इतीर्यते॥

The deep sleep aspect of deep sleep When the object of knowledge, Brahman, whose nature is one of supremely pure consciousness, pervades the whole field of the intellect and all that takes place in it, there is nothing left except the concept of one-ness. That is the deep sleep aspect of deep sleep.

Verse 958

958

पर-ब्रह्मवद् आभाति निर्विकारैक-रूपिणी। सर्वावस्थासु धारैका तुर्याख्या परिकीर्तिता॥

The fourth in relation to the other three When the modifications of the mind remain ever the same, and when the flow of awareness remains unchanged through all the levels of consciousness, and when it assumes one unchanging pattern, the pattern of the supreme Brahman, that is the turya or the transcendent. It is called the fourth in relation to the other three.

Verse 959

959

इत्यवस्था-समुल्लासं विमृशन् मुच्यते सुखी। शुभेच्छादि-त्रयं भूमि-भेदाभेद-युतं स्मृतम्॥

Those who become liberated by virtue of the splendour that animates the various stages of awareness are indeed happy. Beginning with the good intention in the first three of the seven steps in yoga, there is a sense of difference, as well as of non-difference. The first three steps of yoga may together be compared to the waking stage of consciousness. Just as people are aware of the sense of difference in the world, the yogī also has a proper knowledge of difference, though he is firmly convinced of non-difference.

Verse 960

960

यथावद् भेद-बुद्ध्येदं जगज्-जाग्रद् इतीर्यते। अद्वैते स्थैर्यम् आयाते द्वैते च प्रशमं गते॥

Where there is an unshaken perception of the sense of one-ness and where all duality has come to an end, the yogī rises to the fourth plane of consciousness. It may be likened to the dream stage.

Verse 961

961

पश्यन्ति स्वप्नवल् लोकं तुर्य-भूमि-सुयोगतः। पञ्चमीं भूमिम् आरुह्य सुषुप्ति-पद-नामिकाम्॥

When the yogī rises to the fifth plane of consciousness it is as though he has entered the stage of deep sleep. All the special traits of the yogī vanish, and he remains absorbed in non-duality.

Verse 962

962

शान्ताशेष-विशेषांशस् तिष्ठेद् अद्वैत-मात्रके। अन्तर्-मुखतया नित्यं षष्ठीं भूमिम् उपाश्रितः॥

On rising to the sixth plane of consciousness the mind of the yogī is ever turned inward, and to all appearance it looks as though out of fatigue he has fallen into the stage of deep sleep.

Verse 963

963

परिश्रान्ततया गाढ-निद्रालुर् इव लक्ष्यते। कुर्वन्न् अभ्यासम् एतस्यां भूम्यां सम्यग् विवासनः॥

As a result of constant practice in the sixth place, the best among the yogīs becomes completely freed from all desires, and he gradually ascends to the seventh plane of consciousness. This is the turya or the transcendent.

Verse 964

964

विदेह-मुक्तिर् एवात्र तुर्यातीत-दशोच्यते॥

Videha-mukti Even beyond the state of turya is the state of videha-mukti.

Verse 965

965

यत्र नासन् न सच् चापि नाहं नाप्यनहङ्-कृतिः। केवलं क्षीण-मनन आस्तेऽद्वैतेऽतिनिर्भयः॥

In that state there is neither existence nor non-existence. There is neither the ego nor the non-ego. The yogī then becomes totally devoid of fear, and rising free of all intellectual ideations, remains in absolute non-duality.

Verse 966

966

अन्तः-शून्यो बहिः-शून्यः शून्य-कुम्भ इवाम्बरे। अन्तः-पूर्णो बहिः-पूर्णः पूर्ण-कुम्भ इवार्णवे॥

His state may be likened to that of an empty pot set in empty space, so that there is nothing but emptiness within and without; or to a full vessel set in the full ocean, so that there is nothing but fullness inside and out.

Verse 967

967

यथा-स्थितम् इदं सर्वं व्यवहारवतोऽपि च। अस्तं गतं स्थितं व्योम स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

Although the jīvan-mukta is apparently engaged in the various activities of life as usual he is not affected by them. In his view the vast space around and the universe with it have ceased to be. Of such a person it is said that he is liberated in life.

Verse 968

968

नोदेति नास्तम् आयाति सुख-दुःखे मनः-प्रभा। यथा-प्राप्त-स्थितिर् यस्य स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person in whose mind the feelings of pleasure and pain neither well up into a crest nor fall into a trough, and whose life is in harmony with circumstances, is indeed liberated in life.

Verse 969

969

यो जागर्त्ति सुषुप्ति-स्थो यस्य जाग्रन् न विद्यते। यस्य निर्वासनो बोधः स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person who is awake in the spirit, even when he is laid asleep in the flesh and who has no other waking state as such, and who is free from all residual desires, is indeed said to be liberated in life.

Verse 970

970

राग-द्वेष-भयादीनाम् अनुरूपं चरन्न् अपि। योऽन्तर् व्योमवद् अत्यच्छः स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person whose heart is as pure as space, even though his actions seem to be in conformity (like us) with the ordinary feelings of likes and dislikes, as well as, of fear and the like, is indeed liberated in life.

Verse 971

971

यस्य नाहङ्-कृतो भावो बुद्धिर् यस्य न लिप्यते। कुर्वतोऽकुर्वतो वापि स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person whose mind is free from egoism, and from attachment, whether (like us) at work or at rest, is said to be liberated in life.

Verse 972

972

यः समस्तार्थ-जालेषु व्यवहार्यपि शीतलः। परार्थेष्विव पूर्णात्मा स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person whose nature is as all-pervading as Brahman, and who even though (like us) he is engaged in the various activities of life remains free of all attachment as though they were the affairs of other people, is indeed liberated in life.

Verse 973

973

द्वैत-वर्जित-चिन्-मात्रे पदे परम-पावने। अक्षुब्ध-चित्त-विश्रान्तः स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person who is endowed with an unperturbed mind, and who is at ease, after having attained the goal, Brahman, who is the greatest of purifiers, and who finds peace in the pure consciousness which is devoid of all duality, is indeed liberated in life.

Verse 974

974

इदं जगद् अयं सोऽयं दृश्य-जातम् अवास्तवम्। यस्य चित्ते न स्फुरति स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person in whose mind all concepts such as ‘this world’ or ‘this person’, or ‘he is such’ have completely vanished, for they relate to unreal objects, is indeed said to be liberated in life.

Verse 975

975

चिद्-आत्माहं परात्माहं निर्गुणोऽहं परात् परः। आत्म-मात्रेण यस् तिष्ठेत् स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person who lives as the pure Ātman, and who knows ‘I am the Ātman, whose nature is pure consciousness’, ‘I am the supreme Ātman’ and ‘I am superior to the superiors’, is said to be liberated in life.

Verse 976

976

देह-त्रयातिरिक्तोऽहं शुद्ध-चैतन्यम् अस्म्यहम्। ब्रह्माहम् इति यस्यान्तः स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person who knows at heart ‘I am beyond the three bodies – the gross, the subtle and the causal’ – and who knows ‘I am pure consciousness; I am Brahman’ is indeed said to be liberated in life.

Verse 977

977

यस्य देहादिकं नास्ति यस्य ब्रह्मेति निश्चयः। परमानन्द-पूर्णो यः स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person who is devoid of the consciousness of body or the mind, and who has the firm conviction that all this is Brahman, and who is full of Supreme Bliss, is said to be liberated in life.

Verse 978

978

अहं ब्रह्मास्म्यहं ब्रह्मास्म्यहं ब्रह्मेति निश्चयः। चिद् अहं चिद् अहं चेति स जीवन्-मुक्त उच्यते॥

That person who is firmly convinced, ‘I am Brahman, and nothing else; I am pure consciousness, and nothing else’ is indeed liberated in life.

Verse 979

979

जीवन्-मुक्ति-पदं त्यक्त्वा स्व-देहे कालसात्कृते। विशत्यदेह-मुक्तित्वं पवनोऽस्पन्दताम् इव॥

In the course of time, when the person who has attained liberation in life gives up his physical body, he enters videha-mukti, and is devoid of the consciousness of the body. His state may be compared to the wind becoming calm.

Verse 980

980

ततस् तत् सम्बभूवासौ यद् गिराम् अप्यगोचरम्। यच् छून्य-वादिनां शून्यं ब्रह्म ब्रह्म-विदां च यत्॥

One then enters that state which is beyond thought and expression. It is referred to as the void by those who believe in the void, and as Brahman by the knowers of Brahman.

Verse 981

981

विज्ञानं विज्ञान-विदां मलानां च मलात्मकम्। पुरुषः साङ्ख्य-दृष्टीनाम् ईश्वरो योग-वादिनाम्॥

It is knowledge, for the upholders of the doctrine of knowledge, is dirt no bigger than the body for some Jaina mendicants, it is the Puruṣa for the Sāṅkhya school and it is Īśvara, for the followers of Yoga doctrine.

Verse 982

982

शिवः शैवागम-स्थानां कालः कालैक-वादिनाम्। यत् सर्व-शास्त्र-सिद्धान्तं यत् सर्व-हृदयानुगम्। यत् सर्वं सर्वगं वस्तु तत् तत्त्वं तद् असौ स्थितः॥

It is Śiva for the followers of Śaiva āgama. It is Kāla or time for the astrologers who consider time to be supreme. But all the Śāstras agree upon this that it is the Supreme Truth, which enters the heart of all, is in all and all pervading. It is that Supreme Reality that the liberated one is established.

Verse 983

983

ब्रह्मैवाहं चिद् एवाहम् एवं वापि न चिन्त्यते। चिन्-मात्रेणेव यस् तिष्ठेद् विदेहो मुक्त एव सः॥

One who is free from such thoughts as ‘I am Brahman’ and ‘I am knowledge alone’ is established in pure consciousness only and is undoubtedly liberated in life.

Verse 984

984

यस्य प्रपञ्च-भानं न ब्रह्माकारम् अपीह न। अतीतातीत-भावो यो विदेहो मुक्त एव सः॥

One who has gone beyond the reach of the mind, to whom the world does not exist, to whom Brahman is not an object of knowledge, and who has cast off all the vāsanās or past impressions is liberated, and is undoubtedly free from body consciousness.

Verse 985

985

चित्त-वृत्तेर् अतीतो यश् चित्त-वृत्त्यावभासकः। चित्त-वृत्ति-विहीनो यो विदेहो मुक्त एव सः॥

One who has gone even beyond the reach of thought, to whom the reflex ideas that thought presents are no longer valid, and who has gained knowledge by direct intuition is indeed liberated, and is free from the consciousness of the body.

Verse 986

986

जीवात्मेति परात्मेति सर्व-चिन्ता-विवर्जितः। सर्व-सङ्कल्प-हीनात्मा विदेहो मुक्त एव सः॥

One who no longer thinks of the individual, the jīvātman as distinct from the Supreme, the Paramātman and who has lost in the consciousness of the Ātman all saṅkalpas or mental resolves, such a one is indeed liberated and is free of the consciousness of the body.

Verse 987

987

ओङ्कार-वाच्य-हीनात्मा सर्व-वाच्य-विवर्जितः। अवस्था-त्रय-हीनात्मा विदेहो मुक्त एव सः॥

One who has transcended the three levels of consciousness, who has gone beyond all that is connoted by means of words, and who has gone even beyond what is indicated directly by the sacred symbol ‘Om’ is indeed liberated, and is free of the consciousness of the body.

Verse 988

988

अहि-निर्ल्वयनी-सर्प-निर्मोको जीव-वर्जितः। वल्मीके पतितस् तिष्ठेत् तं सर्पो नाभिमन्यते॥

Just as a snake does not regard as its own the slough which it has cast off, and which lies mouldering on an ant-hill, so also the liberated one does not identify himself with either the gross body of the subtle body. ((BrhUEng.4.4.7) (Bṛhad-Āraṇyaka Upaniṣad))

Verse 989

989

एवं स्थूलं च सूक्ष्मं च शरीरं नाभिमन्यते। प्रत्यग्-ज्ञान-शिखि-ध्वस्ते मिथ्या-ज्ञाने सहेतुके॥

All false ideas have become burnt up in the fire of knowledge. What remains is pure consciousness alone, and the Śruti points it out by the expression ‘Not Such; Nor such’.

Verse 990

990

नेति नेतीत्यरूपत्वाद् अशरीरो भवत्ययम्। विश्वश्च तैजसश्चैव प्राज्ञश्चेति च ते त्रयम्॥

The Ātman has neither form nor shape. To know that and to identify oneself with the Ātman is to go beyond the state of an embodied being. Thus the attributes of Viśva, Taijasa, and Prājña, along with their adjuncts drop off.

Verse 991

991

विराड् हिरण्य-गर्भश्च ईश्वरश्चेति ते त्रयम्। ब्रह्माण्डं चैव पिण्डाण्डं लोका भूर्-आदयः क्रमात्॥

The knower of the Ātman goes beyond the concepts of Virāṭ, Hiraṇya-garbha and Īśvara, for they too are aspects of the Ātman when taken along with the adjuncts only. To the knower of the Ātman the whole world and its adjuncts merge in the Ātman.

Verse 992

992

स्व-स्वोपाधि-लयाद् एव लीयन्ते प्रत्यग्-आत्मनि। तूष्णीम् एव ततस् तूष्णीं तूष्णीं सत्यं न किञ्चन॥

In this way, when the adjuncts of the Piṇḍāṇḍa and the Brahmāṇḍa, the microcosm and the macrocosm, merge in the Ātman; all that remains is a grand silence. That silence alone is real; and there is nothing else so real as that.

Verse 993

993

काल-भेदो वस्तु-भेदो देश-भेदः स्व-भेदकः। किञ्चिद्-भेदो न तस्यास्ति किञ्चिद् वापि न विद्यते॥

All distinctions of time, space, and the nature of things have ceased. Similarly all the distinctions within oneself have also ceased. Where there are no distinctions at all, in reality, there are none to be perceived.

Verse 994

994

जीवेश्वरेति-वाक्ये च वेद-शास्त्रेष्वहं त्विति। इदं चैतन्यम् एवेत्यहं चैतन्यम् इत्यपि॥

It is true that the Śruti speaks of the Jīva and the Īśvara, but the knower of Brahman goes beyond what is indicated by the mere words of the Śruti. To him even the idea ‘I am the pure consciousness’, ‘The pure consciousness is myself’ does not arise.

Verse 995

995

इति निश्चय-शून्यो यो विदेहो मुक्त एव सः। ब्रह्मैव विद्यते साक्षाद् वस्तुतोऽवस्तुतोऽपि च॥

When the ‘I’ consciousness does not at all arise, the liberated one is undoubtedly devoid of body consciousness. Such a one exists as Brahman, and also otherwise.

Verse 996

996

तद् विद्या-विषयं ब्रह्म सत्य-ज्ञान-सुखात्मकम्। शान्तं च तद्-अतीतं च परं ब्रह्म तद् उच्यते॥

Brahman is an object of knowledge when Brahman is endowed with the attributes of existence-knowledge-bliss. But the Supreme Brahman is not an object of knowledge, and transcends knowledge. ((TaitU.2.1.1) (English Upaniṣad – Taittiriya))

Verse 997

997

सिद्धान्तोऽध्यात्म-शास्त्राणां सर्वापह्नव एव हि। नाविद्यास्तीह नो माया शान्तं ब्रह्मैव तद्-विना॥

The siddhānta or the sifted truth in relation to the Adhyātma Śāstra, the knowledge relating to the Ātman, is that Brahman is not an object of knowledge. Consequently, avidyā and Māyā, which are also objects of knowledge have no being. All that is, is the supreme tranquil Brahman.

Verse 998

998

प्रियेषु स्वेषु सुकृतम् अप्रियेषु च दुष्कृतम्। विसृज्य ध्यान-योगेन ब्रह्माप्येति सनातनम्॥

The knower of Brahman thus leaves a legacy of good deeds to those who befriend him, while his evil deeds go to those who think ill. After having realized the eternal Brahman, he becomes one with Brahman in profound meditation. ((BrSEng.3.2.26) (Brahma Sūtras Eng.))

Verse 999

999

यावद् यावच् च सद्-बुद्धे स्वयं सन्त्यज्यतेऽखिलम्। तावत् तावत् परानन्दः परमात्मैव शिष्यते॥

O wise one! know that it is by giving up all that which is unreal, that one attains what is real. In this way, one realizes the Supreme Being, and attains the Supreme Bliss. That is what is left over, that is the residual value.

Verse 1000

1000

यत्र यत्र मृतो ज्ञानी परमाक्षरवित् सदा। परे ब्रह्मणि लीयेत न तस्योत्क्रान्तिर् इष्यते॥

At whatever place the knower of the Supreme Brahman may cast off his body, he invariably merges in Brahman. To him there is no going, and no return, in order to assume a fresh body. ((BrSEng.4.1.14) (Brahma Sūtras Eng.))

Verse 1001

1001

यद् यत् स्वाभिमतं वस्तु तत् त्यजन् मोक्षम् अश्नुते। असङ्कल्पेन शस्त्रेण छिन्नं चित्तम् इदं यदा॥

By giving up all things that are held dear one attains liberation. Desirelessness is therefore the greatest weapon.

Verse 1002

1002

सर्वं सर्वगतं शान्तं ब्रह्म सम्पद्यते तदा। इति श्रुत्वा गुरोर् वाक्यं शिष्यस् तु छिन्न-संशयः॥

It is with that weapon that one cuts asunder all the knots of the heart. One then realizes Brahman, all in all, all-pervading, and tranquil.

Verse 1003

1003

ज्ञात-ज्ञेयः सम्प्रणम्य सद्गुरोश् चरणाम्बुजम्। स तेन समनुज्ञातो ययौ निर्मुक्त-बन्धनः॥

After having heard all that the Guru had to say, the disciple came to know what he should do. All his doubts were dispelled. He had become free of bondage. He prostrated himself at the lotus feet of the good preceptor. With his permission, he took leave of him.

Verse 1004

1004

गुरुर् एष सदानन्द-सिन्धौ निर्मग्न-मानसः। पावयन् वसुधां सर्वां विचचार निरुत्तरः॥

As for the Guru, he whose mind was steeped in the bliss of the divine consciousness, he too left the place, in order to roam over the Earth and purity it by his silence.

Verse 1005

1005

इत्याचार्यस्य शिष्यस्य संवादेनात्म-लक्षणम्। निरूपितं मुमुक्षूणां सुख-बोधोपपत्तये॥

In this discourse between the Guru and the disciple the real nature of the Ātman has been brought out. It has been set down in order that it might be easily understood by other aspirants after liberation.

Verse 1006

1006

सर्व-वेदान्त-सिद्धान्त-सार-सङ्ग्रह-नामकः। ग्रन्थोऽयं हृदय-ग्रन्थि-विच्छित्त्यै रचितः सताम्॥

This work entitled ‘The Quintessence of Vedānta’ has been specially composed in order that it might help other aspirants after wisdom in dispelling the doubts that might arise in their hearts.
